# H&R Block Software 2025 (Premium) — chronological walkthrough

Generated by `tools/gen-walkthrough.py` from the per-screen captures in `screens/` (PNG + JSON: full text, every input with
field id/label/value, buttons, help links) plus native-shell captures in `shell/` (whole window PNG + OCR of the
refund meter and the contextual FAQ panel). Household: fictional MFJ couple Jordan & Casey Sample (IL) + son Riley.
Screen numbers are capture order; a number may show the same screen before/after an action (noted).

Type legend: Intro/Interstitial · Question (Y/N) · Question (choice) · Entry · Checklist · Gateway/Hub · Summary/List ·
Outcome/Eligibility result · Help pop-up (h*) · Variant/What-if (v*) · Review/Error-check · Upsell.

### 002 — How do you want to import your taxes from last year?

- **Image:** `screens/002-start-return-clicked.png`
- **Type:** Question (choice)
- **Content:** How do you want to import your taxes from last year? I used H&R Block software or Turbo Tax software and have my data file. (recommended) Giving us your data file is the fastest, most accurate way to import your info. If you used H&R Block software, search for a file ending with the extension .T24 If you used TurboTax, the file we need ends in .tax2024 I have a PDF copy of my 2024 return.
- **Controls (2):** `rb_LastYearsTaxes` radio [x] "I used H&R Block software or Turbo Tax software and have my data file."; `rb_LastYearsTaxes` radio [ ] "I have a PDF copy of my 2024 return."
- **Buttons:** Back, Skip, Next
- **Actions taken to reach/fill:** `[["clickText", "Start a Return"]]`
- **Notes:** Prior-year import: radio choice between data file (.T24/.tax2024 — explicitly accepts TurboTax files, '(recommended)' bold) and PDF of 2024 return. Three nav buttons: Back / Skip / Next. Left panel switches to contextual FAQs.

### 009 — Married Filing Jointly Most people who were married as December 31, 2025 will ch

- **Image:** `screens/009-help-mfj-definition.png`
- **Type:** Help pop-up (h*)
- **Help text:** Married Filing Jointly Most people who were married as December 31, 2025 will choose to file jointly with their spouse. The other options are to file separate returns, or, if you qualify, to file as head of household. If you file jointly, you and your spouse report all your income and expenses together on a single federal return, and are jointly responsible for the payment of the tax due. This usually yields a lower overall tax bill than filing separate returns. To qualify for head of household filing status, you'll need to have kids and live apart from your spouse. To explore head of household filing status, click Guide Me.
- **Notes:** MFJ definition (hMFJ) — plain-language paragraph, ends by pointing to Guide Me to explore HOH.

### 021 — Why does this matter? If you were affected by a federal disaster, you might qual

- **Image:** `screens/021-help-disaster-why-matter.png`
- **Type:** Help pop-up (h*)
- **Help text:** Why does this matter? If you were affected by a federal disaster, you might qualify for some tax relief. You may be entitled to a later filing deadline. Learn more here: IRS Tax Relief in Disaster Situations | H&R Block. If this applies to you, be sure to vist the Deductions section of the interview and select I had a casualty or theft loss. Provisions related to Qualified Federal Disaster Losses have been extended. The Federal Disaster Tax Relief Act of 2023 extended some special rules. Qualified federal disaster losses have been extended to include those declared by the President between Jan. 1, 2020 and Sep. 2, 2025 (previously Feb. 25, 2021). Your casualty event must have started between Dec. 28, 2019 and Jul. 4, 2025, and ended no later than Aug. 3, 2025.
- **Notes:** 'Why does this matter?' disaster help — long paragraph with dates, cross-references the Deductions section, external hrblock.com link.

### 048 — Special Situations Your employer has moved. Even if your employer has moved to a

- **Image:** `screens/048-help-w2-special-situations.png`
- **Type:** Help pop-up (h*)
- **Help text:** Special Situations Your employer has moved. Even if your employer has moved to an address that's different from your W-2, tell us the address that's on the W-2. If you don't, and you e-file, your refund could be delayed. You can't fit the address into the field. We need your employer's address for e-filing. Type in as much as you can, exactly as it appears on your W-2, and your return will be accepted. If you're planning to e-file, you shouldn't try to abbreviate the address. Doing so will create a discrepancy.

### 095 — Wages If the amount of total wages shown here is different from what you entered

- **Image:** `screens/095-help-explain-amount-wages.png`
- **Type:** Help pop-up (h*)
- **Help text:** Wages If the amount of total wages shown here is different from what you entered from your W-2(s), it may also include income entered or calculated elsewhere in your return. Other types of income that may be included in your total wages include: Tips you earned in your job that weren't included in your W-2 Taxable employer-provided dependent care benefits Disability income received before minimum retirement age (Form 1099-R) Reimbursements from your employer that exceed your expenses Moving reimbursements that exceed your expenses Income earned as a household employee Sick or disability payments Foreign-source wages Excess salary deferrals
- **Notes:** EXPLAIN AMOUNT (Wages) is a STATIC generic help text listing what else might be included in wages — not a breakdown of the user's actual 137,000.

### 134 — How can I create a list of items to add up and go into a field? To create an ite

- **Image:** `screens/134-help-faq-itemized-list-v-screen.png`
- **Type:** Variant/What-if (v*)
- **Help text:** How can I create a list of items to add up and go into a field? To create an itemized list of items that will add up to go into a field: Go to the Edit menu and select Add Itemized List. A new blank itemized list appears with a title that uses the form and line number to which it belongs. Type the description of the first item in your list. Press the Tab key to move to the next column. Enter the amount for the first item. Press the Tab key to move to the second item, and so on. When you're done entering items, click OK to close the itemized list. The sum of all the items you entered will carry to the appropriate field.
- **Notes:** Left-panel FAQ (v-screen vItemizedList) opened via the native panel: explains Edit > Add Itemized List — a supporting-detail list whose sum carries to any field (power-user feature).

### 138 — Why Itemize? Usually, it's best to claim the standard deduction when it's more t

- **Image:** `screens/138-help-why-itemize.png`
- **Type:** Help pop-up (h*)
- **Help text:** Why Itemize? Usually, it's best to claim the standard deduction when it's more than your itemized deductions. However, in some states, you might want to try it both ways to see which is best overall: In Georgia, Oklahoma, and Virginia, you can itemize on your state return only if you itemize on your federal return. In Massachusetts, you can claim medical expenses only if you itemized on your federal return.
- **Notes:** 'Why Itemize?' — state interactions (GA/OK/VA itemize-only-if-federal; MA medical).

- **Content:** Bunching Your Deductions Advice This year, your itemized deductions are less than your standard deduction. In future years, you can bunch your deductions to have more expenses you can itemize. Here are some ways you can bunch your deductions: Make charitable donations every two or three years so your total deduction is higher in those years. Pay state and local taxes the same years you make your charitable donations. So, you might pay your taxes in December some years and in January other years.
